- reading: For $\mathcal H$ and, for each fixed $g\in\mathcal G$, $\mathcal T_g\mathcal H$, the empirical-process suprema used in the critical-radius analysis are measurable under their respective input distributions. → `PinRDIVMeasurabilityReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:930`)
- reading: For every $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$, the empirical RDIV minimum is attained and $\hat h_\lambda$ is a measurable selected minimizer of $L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]$ over $h\in\mathcal H$. → `PinRDIVMinimaReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:936`)
- reading: The displayed discrepancy inequality holds on $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IR}}$, with $\mathbb P(\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IR}})\geqslant1-\xi$, simultaneously for every $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ,2]$. → `PinIterRDIVEnvelopeReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterRDIV.lean:493`)
- reading: For every preliminary sample size $n\geqslant1$, the empirical log likelihood $g\mapsto\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{pre}}[\log g(X\mid Z)]$ attains its maximum over $\mathcal G$ almost surely, and $\hat g\in\mathcal G$ is a measurable maximizer selected using only $I_{\mathrm{pre}}$. → `MLEMaximizerSelected` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:343`)
- condition of the paper: \label{asp:range} We have $ r_{0} \in \mathcal{R}(\mathcal{T}) :=\{\mathcal{T} h:h\in \tilde{\mathcal{H}}\}$. → `RangeCondition` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:136`)
- condition of the paper: \label{asp:source_condition} Problem (\ref{eq:lin_inv_prob}) satisfies the $ \beta $-source condition for some $ \beta >0$, i.e., there exists $ w_{0} \in \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ such that the minimum-norm solution of (\ref{eq:lin_inv_prob}) satisfies $ h_{0} =\left(\mathcal{T}^{*}\mathcal{T}\right)^{\beta /2} w_{0}$. → `BetaSourceCondition` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:203`)
- condition of the paper: \label{asp:closeness_deepiv} The minimum-norm solution $h_0$ lies in $\mathcal H$. → `H0MemHypothesisClass` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:210`)
- condition of the paper: \label{asp:re_cd} The conditional density $g_0$ belongs to $\mathcal G$, whose elements are conditional densities with respect to a common dominating measure. → `DensityRealizable` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:75`)
- condition of the paper: For fixed constants $c_0,C_G>0$, \[ g_0(x\mid z)\geqslant c_0, \qquad \sup_{g\in\mathcal G}\|g\|_\infty\leqslant C_G. \] → `DensityBounds` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:86`)
- condition of the paper: For a prescribed $\beta$-independent accuracy bound $\delta_{n,r}=\delta_{n,r}(\xi)\geqslant0$, the nuisance satisfies \[ \|\hat r-r_0\|_{L_2(Z)}\leqslant \delta_{n,r} \] with probability at least $1-\xi$. → `PilotRegressionRate` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:100`)
- condition of the paper: For a fixed $B<\infty$, $|Y|\leqslant B$ almost surely → `OutcomeBounded` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:115`)
- condition of the paper: every $h\in\mathcal H$ satisfies $|h(x)|\leqslant B$ on the common support of the conditional densities → `HypothesisClassBounded` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:121`)
- condition of the paper: $|\hat r(z)|\leqslant B$ → `PilotBounded` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:130`)
- condition of the paper: The classes below are measurable. → `RDIVClassesMeasurable` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:183`)
- condition of the paper: The $\beta$-independent sequence $\delta_{n,\mathcal G}=\delta_{n,\mathcal G}(\xi)$ upper-bounds the critical radius of $\mathcal G_0$ → `under` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:251`)
- condition of the paper: $\delta_{n,\mathcal H}=\delta_{n,\mathcal H}(\eta_n)$ is a common upper bound on the critical radii of \[ \mathcal H \quad\text{and}\quad \mathcal T_g\mathcal H:=\{\mathcal T_g h:h\in\mathcal H\}, \qquad g\in\mathcal G, \] under their respective input distributions. → `HypothesisCriticalRadiusBound` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:266`)
- condition of the paper: Moreover, \[ \delta_{n,\mathcal G}\geqslant C\sqrt{\frac{\log\log(en)+\log(1/\xi)}{n}}, \qquad \delta_{n,\mathcal H}\geqslant C\sqrt{\frac{\log\log(en)+\log(1/\eta_n)}{n}}. \] → `RDIVRadiusFloor` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:281`)
- condition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:delta_deepiv} \begin{aligned} \delta_{n,\xi}&:=\sqrt{\frac{\log((K_n+1)/\xi)}{n}}, &\qquad \delta_n&\geqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}+\delta_{n,\mathcal H}+\delta_{n,\xi},\\ \nu_n&\geqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}+\delta_{n,r}+\delta_{n,\xi}, &\qquad \delta_n+\nu_n&=o(1). \end{aligned} \end{equation} → `RDIVPublicScaleCalibration` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:619`)
- condition of the paper: We assume these empirical minima are attained and measurable. → `RDIVMinimaAttained` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:510`)
- condition of the paper: In the following analyses, $\delta_n$ and $\nu_n$ denote deterministic upper bounds multiplied by fixed constants large enough to absorb the concentration and stability constants. → `CalibratedValidBounds` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:387`)
- condition of the paper: These multipliers may depend on the boundedness, density-ratio, and mean-square-continuity constants, but are independent of $n$, $\lambda$, and the unknown source parameter. → `CalibrationSourceFree` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:398`)
- condition of the paper: They are fixed before fitting the candidate estimators. → `CalibrationDeterministic` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:406`)
- condition of the paper: \label{asp:iter_realizability_rdiv} For every $n$ for which $\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ$ is defined, $h_{j,\lambda}^*\in\mathcal H$ for $j=1,\ldots,t$ and $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ,2]$. → `IterRealizableRDIV` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterRDIVSetup.lean:118`)
- condition of the paper: We assume that each empirical minimum is attained and that the selected minimizers are measurable. → `IterRDIVMinimaAttained` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterRDIVSetup.lean:132`)
- definition of the paper: Fix an integer $t\geqslant1$, chosen independently of the data and the source parameter $\beta$, and initialize \[ h_{0,\lambda}^*=\hat h_{0,\lambda}=0. \] For $j=1,\ldots,t$, define the population and empirical iterates by \begin{align} h_{j,\lambda}^* &=\arg\min_{h\in\widetilde{\mathcal H}} \left\{\|\mathcal T h-r_0\|^2+\lambda\|h-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*\|^2\right\}, \label{eq:iter_population}, \\ \hat h_{j,\lambda} &=\arg\min_{h\in\mathcal H} \left\{L_n(h)+\lambda\|h-\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}\|_{2,n}^2\right\}. \label{eq:iter_rdiv_estimator} \end{align} → `iterativeRecursion` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterRDIVSetup.lean:70`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:rdiv_projected_loss} D_n(h)=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{cal}} [(\hat r(Z)-\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z))^2]. \end{equation} → `rdivCalibrationStat` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:442`)
- definition of the paper: Let $a_t\geqslant1$ be a deterministic constant that depends only on the prescribed iteration count $t$ and fixed population quantities. Define \[ q_t=\min\{\beta+1,2t\}, \qquad s_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}=a_t\delta_n^2. \] We then use \begin{equation}\label{eq:iter_rdiv_common_scales} A_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}(\lambda)=\frac{s_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}}{\lambda}+\nu_n^2, \qquad V_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}(\lambda)=\frac{s_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}}{\lambda^2}, \end{equation} → `iterRdivNoiseLevel` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterRDIVSetup.lean:55`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound} D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda}) \leqslant A_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}(\lambda) +C_{\mathrm{IR}}\|w_0\|^2\lambda^{q_t}, \qquad \lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ,2]. \end{equation} → `IterRDIVDiscrepancyEnvelope` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterRDIVSetup.lean:232`)
- definition of the paper: For a sufficiently small fixed $c_{\mathrm{IR}}>0$, let $\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ$ be the first grid point no larger than \[ r_{n,t,\beta}^{\mathrm{IR}} =c_{\mathrm{IR}}\max\left\{ (s_{n,t}^{\mathrm R})^{1/(q_t+1)},\nu_n^{2/q_t} \right\}. \] → `iterRdivOraclePoint` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterRDIVSetup.lean:107`)
- definition of the paper: Fix $\lambda_0=2$ and $\rho\in[1/2,1)$, independent of $n$, and set \[ K_n=\left\lceil\frac{\log(2n^2)}{\log(1/\rho)}\right\rceil, \qquad \Lambda_n=\{\lambda_k=2\rho^k:k=0,\ldots,K_n\}. \] → `regGrid` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/Discrepancy.lean:33`)
- definition of the paper: For $g\in\mathcal G$, write \[ (\mathcal T_g h)(z)=\int h(x)g(x\mid z)\,\mu(dx). \] → `densityOperator` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:18`)
- definition of the paper: Define the likelihood-transform class \[ \mathcal G_0= \left\{(x,z)\mapsto \sqrt{\frac{g(x\mid z)+g_0(x\mid z)}{2g_0(x\mid z)}}: g\in\mathcal G\right\}. \] → `likelihoodTransformClass` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:25`)
- definition of the paper: Let $\eta_n=\xi/(K_n+1)$. → `gridConfidence` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/Discrepancy.lean:47`)
- definition of the paper: On $I_{\mathrm{pre}}$, fit \[ \hat g=\arg\max_{g\in\mathcal G}\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{pre}}[\log g(X\mid Z)], \qquad (\hat{\mathcal T}h)(z)=\int h(x)\hat g(x\mid z)\,\mu(dx), \] and a regression estimate $\hat r$ of $r_0$ from $Y$ on $Z$. → `RDIVPreliminary` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:397`)
- definition of the paper: The RDIV fit is \[ L_n(h)=\mathbb E_n[(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z))^2], \qquad \hat h_\lambda=\arg\min_{h\in\mathcal H}\{L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]\}. \] → `IsRDIVEstimator` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:491`)
- definition of the paper: For the $t(K_n+1)$ fitted iterates, set \[ \eta_{n,t}=\frac{\xi}{t(K_n+1)} . \] → `iterGridConfidence` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/Discrepancy.lean:56`)
- definition of the paper: Let $X$ and $Z$ be two $W$-measurable random variables, and let $ \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $ \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be closed linear subspaces of $ L_{2}( X)$ and $ L_{2}( Z)$, respectively, where $L_2(X)$ denotes square-integrable functions of $X$. Let $\mathcal{H} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $\mathcal{Q} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be subsets of these function spaces. → `IVWorld` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:22`)
- condition of the paper: For the conditional fitting argument in Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}, use the following measurable-version convention. Let $Q$ denote the law of one observation $W$, let $a$ denote the preliminary observations, and put $Q_n^{\mathbf w}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n\delta_{w_i}$ for a fresh fitting tuple $\mathbf w=(w_1,\ldots,w_n)$. Define \[ \ell_{\lambda,a}(h;w)=\{Y(w)-(\hat{\mathcal T}_a h)(Z(w))\}^2+\lambda h(X(w))^2, \qquad S_{n,\lambda,q}(a,\mathbf w)=\sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\|h-h_0\|\leqslant q}}\left|(Q_n^{\mathbf w}-Q)\{\ell_{\lambda,a}(h)-\ell_{\lambda,a}(h_0)\}\right|. \] For each $n\geqslant1$, $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$ and positive rational $q$, $S_{n,\lambda,q}$ admits an extended nonnegative version jointly measurable in the preliminary observations and the fresh fitting tuple. This version agrees with $S_{n,\lambda,q}(a,\cdot)$ for $Q^n$-almost every tuple for almost every preliminary realization, and agrees almost surely with $S_{n,\lambda,q}(I_{\mathrm{pre}},(W_i)_{i\in I_{\mathrm{fit}}})$ at the actual independent fitting block. All losses use the same function representatives as the empirical objective. This is only a measurability and evaluation-coherence convention; no deviation bound, rate, countability or separability is assumed. → `RDIVFittingSupremumVersion` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:228`)
- condition of the paper: For the fitted conditional density in the RDIV preliminary construction, use a coherent measurable version at independent calibration observations. For each $n\geqslant1$, writing $a=I_{\mathrm{pre}}$, the selected density $\hat g_{n,a}(x\mid z)$ admits a nonnegative version $\bar g_n(a,x,z)$ jointly measurable in the preliminary observations, $x$ and $z$. For almost every preliminary realization $a$, for $P_Z$-almost every $z$, the functions $\bar g_n(a,\cdot,z)$ and $\hat g_{n,a}(\cdot\mid z)$ agree $\mu$-almost everywhere. Almost surely, this same agreement holds at every actual calibration observation $Z_i$, $i\in I_{\mathrm{cal}}$. The empirical objectives and selected maximizer remain those defined using the original supplied representatives: this convention does not replace the optimizer. It supplies only measurability and coherence of the fitted density evaluated on the independent calibration sample; it assumes no normalization event, probability inequality, rate, countability or separability. → `RDIVSelectedDensityVersion` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:360`)
- condition of the paper: For the preliminary regression estimate used in the RDIV calibration loss, take a coherent measurable version on the independent calibration sample. For each $n\geqslant1$, writing $a=I_{\mathrm{pre}}$, the selected $L_2(Z)$ pilot has a version $\bar r_n(a,z)$ jointly measurable in the preliminary observations and $z$. For almost every preliminary realization $a$, $\bar r_n(a,z)=\hat r_{n,a}(z)$ for $P_Z$-almost every $z$. Almost surely, the same equality holds simultaneously at every actual calibration observation $Z_i$, $i\in I_{\mathrm{cal}}$, for the supplied representatives used in $D_n$. This is only a measurability and evaluation-coherence convention. It imposes no exact joint measurability on every supplied representative, does not replace an empirical objective or selected optimizer, and supplies no boundedness event, probability inequality, error rate, countability or separability assumption. The existing pilot accuracy and envelope assumptions remain unchanged. → `RDIVSelectedPilotVersion` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:380`)
- condition of the paper: Use the following well-definedness convention for the likelihood-transform critical-radius and concentration arguments in Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv} and Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}, separately from the existing hypothesis-class, density-image and fitted-process conventions. Let $Q$ be the true joint law of $(X,Z)$, let $\mathcal G_0$ be the likelihood-transform class already defined in Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv}, and put \[ \mathcal A_r=\{f\in\operatorname{star}(\mathcal G_0-\mathcal G_0):\|f\|_{L_2(Q)}\leqslant r\}. \] For every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $r>0$, the three processes \[ \sup_{f\in\mathcal G_0}|(Q_n-Q)f|,\qquad \sup_{f\in\mathcal A_r}|(Q_n-Q)f|,\qquad \sup_{f\in\mathcal A_r}\left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i f(X_i,Z_i)\right| \] regarded as extended nonnegative quantities, are measurable under $Q^n$ for the first two and under the product of $Q^n$ with the independent Rademacher law for the third. The functions use the same fixed density representatives as the likelihood objective. This convention only makes these empirical processes and their expectations well defined. It supplies no concentration event, deviation inequality, probability bound, rate, countability, separability, or additional statistical restriction. → `RDIVLikelihoodProcessesMeasurable` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:197`)

## Lemma B.10

**Statement.** \label{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds} Under the assumptions of \cref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, for every $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ,2]$, on $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IR}}$, \begin{align*} \|\mathcal T(\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_0)\|^2 &\leqslant C_t \{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}(\lambda)\},\\ \|\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_0\|^2 &\leqslant C_t\left[ V_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}(\lambda) +\{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}(\lambda)\}^{\beta/(\beta+1)} \right], \end{align*} where the noise level and stability bound are defined in \eqref{eq:iter_rdiv_common_scales}.

**Lean.** `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds` — `lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterRDIV.lean:329`

- definition of the paper: Fix $\lambda_0=2$ and $\rho\in[1/2,1)$, independent of $n$, and set \[ K_n=\left\lceil\frac{\log(2n^2)}{\log(1/\rho)}\right\rceil, \qquad \Lambda_n=\{\lambda_k=2\rho^k:k=0,\ldots,K_n\}. \] → `regGrid` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/Discrepancy.lean:33`)

- definition of the paper: Define the likelihood-transform class \[ \mathcal G_0= \left\{(x,z)\mapsto \sqrt{\frac{g(x\mid z)+g_0(x\mid z)}{2g_0(x\mid z)}}: g\in\mathcal G\right\}. \] → `likelihoodTransformClass` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:25`)
- definition of the paper: Let $\eta_n=\xi/(K_n+1)$. → `gridConfidence` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/Discrepancy.lean:47`)
- definition of the paper: On $I_{\mathrm{pre}}$, fit \[ \hat g=\arg\max_{g\in\mathcal G}\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{pre}}[\log g(X\mid Z)], \qquad (\hat{\mathcal T}h)(z)=\int h(x)\hat g(x\mid z)\,\mu(dx), \] and a regression estimate $\hat r$ of $r_0$ from $Y$ on $Z$. → `RDIVPreliminary` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:397`)
- definition of the paper: The RDIV fit is \[ L_n(h)=\mathbb E_n[(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z))^2], \qquad \hat h_\lambda=\arg\min_{h\in\mathcal H}\{L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]\}. \] → `IsRDIVEstimator` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:491`)
- definition of the paper: For the $t(K_n+1)$ fitted iterates, set \[ \eta_{n,t}=\frac{\xi}{t(K_n+1)} . \] → `iterGridConfidence` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/Discrepancy.lean:56`)
- definition of the paper: Let $X$ and $Z$ be two $W$-measurable random variables, and let $ \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $ \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be closed linear subspaces of $ L_{2}( X)$ and $ L_{2}( Z)$, respectively, where $L_2(X)$ denotes square-integrable functions of $X$. Let $\mathcal{H} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $\mathcal{Q} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be subsets of these function spaces. → `IVWorld` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:22`)
- condition of the paper: For the conditional fitting argument in Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}, use the following measurable-version convention. Let $Q$ denote the law of one observation $W$, let $a$ denote the preliminary observations, and put $Q_n^{\mathbf w}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n\delta_{w_i}$ for a fresh fitting tuple $\mathbf w=(w_1,\ldots,w_n)$. Define \[ \ell_{\lambda,a}(h;w)=\{Y(w)-(\hat{\mathcal T}_a h)(Z(w))\}^2+\lambda h(X(w))^2, \qquad S_{n,\lambda,q}(a,\mathbf w)=\sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\|h-h_0\|\leqslant q}}\left|(Q_n^{\mathbf w}-Q)\{\ell_{\lambda,a}(h)-\ell_{\lambda,a}(h_0)\}\right|. \] For each $n\geqslant1$, $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$ and positive rational $q$, $S_{n,\lambda,q}$ admits an extended nonnegative version jointly measurable in the preliminary observations and the fresh fitting tuple. This version agrees with $S_{n,\lambda,q}(a,\cdot)$ for $Q^n$-almost every tuple for almost every preliminary realization, and agrees almost surely with $S_{n,\lambda,q}(I_{\mathrm{pre}},(W_i)_{i\in I_{\mathrm{fit}}})$ at the actual independent fitting block. All losses use the same function representatives as the empirical objective. This is only a measurability and evaluation-coherence convention; no deviation bound, rate, countability or separability is assumed. → `RDIVFittingSupremumVersion` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:228`)
- condition of the paper: For the fitted conditional density in the RDIV preliminary construction, use a coherent measurable version at independent calibration observations. For each $n\geqslant1$, writing $a=I_{\mathrm{pre}}$, the selected density $\hat g_{n,a}(x\mid z)$ admits a nonnegative version $\bar g_n(a,x,z)$ jointly measurable in the preliminary observations, $x$ and $z$. For almost every preliminary realization $a$, for $P_Z$-almost every $z$, the functions $\bar g_n(a,\cdot,z)$ and $\hat g_{n,a}(\cdot\mid z)$ agree $\mu$-almost everywhere. Almost surely, this same agreement holds at every actual calibration observation $Z_i$, $i\in I_{\mathrm{cal}}$. The empirical objectives and selected maximizer remain those defined using the original supplied representatives: this convention does not replace the optimizer. It supplies only measurability and coherence of the fitted density evaluated on the independent calibration sample; it assumes no normalization event, probability inequality, rate, countability or separability. → `RDIVSelectedDensityVersion` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:360`)
- condition of the paper: For the preliminary regression estimate used in the RDIV calibration loss, take a coherent measurable version on the independent calibration sample. For each $n\geqslant1$, writing $a=I_{\mathrm{pre}}$, the selected $L_2(Z)$ pilot has a version $\bar r_n(a,z)$ jointly measurable in the preliminary observations and $z$. For almost every preliminary realization $a$, $\bar r_n(a,z)=\hat r_{n,a}(z)$ for $P_Z$-almost every $z$. Almost surely, the same equality holds simultaneously at every actual calibration observation $Z_i$, $i\in I_{\mathrm{cal}}$, for the supplied representatives used in $D_n$. This is only a measurability and evaluation-coherence convention. It imposes no exact joint measurability on every supplied representative, does not replace an empirical objective or selected optimizer, and supplies no boundedness event, probability inequality, error rate, countability or separability assumption. The existing pilot accuracy and envelope assumptions remain unchanged. → `RDIVSelectedPilotVersion` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:380`)
- condition of the paper: Use the following well-definedness convention for the likelihood-transform critical-radius and concentration arguments in Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv} and Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}, separately from the existing hypothesis-class, density-image and fitted-process conventions. Let $Q$ be the true joint law of $(X,Z)$, let $\mathcal G_0$ be the likelihood-transform class already defined in Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv}, and put \[ \mathcal A_r=\{f\in\operatorname{star}(\mathcal G_0-\mathcal G_0):\|f\|_{L_2(Q)}\leqslant r\}. \] For every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $r>0$, the three processes \[ \sup_{f\in\mathcal G_0}|(Q_n-Q)f|,\qquad \sup_{f\in\mathcal A_r}|(Q_n-Q)f|,\qquad \sup_{f\in\mathcal A_r}\left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i f(X_i,Z_i)\right| \] regarded as extended nonnegative quantities, are measurable under $Q^n$ for the first two and under the product of $Q^n$ with the independent Rademacher law for the third. The functions use the same fixed density representatives as the likelihood objective. This convention only makes these empirical processes and their expectations well defined. It supplies no concentration event, deviation inequality, probability bound, rate, countability, separability, or additional statistical restriction. → `RDIVLikelihoodProcessesMeasurable` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:197`)

## Lemma C.1

**Statement.** \label{lemma:concentrate_l} Under \cref{asp:closeness,asp:mean_sq_continuity,asp:critical_radius}, on $\mathcal E_n$, the following inequalities hold simultaneously for all $h\in\mathcal H$: \[ \frac{1}{2} L( h) -O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right) \leqslant L_{n}( h) \leqslant \frac{5}{4} L( h) +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right) . \]

**Conditions and readings, and the Lean definitions that carry them.**

- reading: There exists a fixed $C<\infty$, independent of $f$, $n$, and $\lambda$, such that $\mathbb E[m(W;f)^2]\leqslant C\|f\|^2$ for every $f\in\mathcal F$. → `PinMeanSquareReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:973`)
- reading: The lower bound denoted by $\Omega$ means that there exists a constant $c>0$, independent of $n$ and $\xi$, such that for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $\xi\in(0,1)$, $\tilde\delta_n(\xi)\geqslant c\sqrt{\{\log\log(en)+\log(1/\xi)\}/n}$. → `PinCriticalRadiusOmegaReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:980`)
- condition of the paper: \label{asp:closeness} For all $h \in \mathcal{H}$, $\mathcal T(h_0-h)\in\mathcal F$. → `ClosenessCondition` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:75`)
- condition of the paper: \label{asp:mean_sq_continuity} For all $ f\in \mathcal{F}$, $ \mathbb{E}\left[ m( W;f)^{2}\right] \leqslant O\left( \| f\| ^{2}\right)$. → `MeanSquareContinuity` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:82`)
- condition of the paper: For a fixed $B<\infty$, suppose that $|m(W;f)|$, $|h(X)|$, and $|f(Z)|$ are a.s. bounded by $B$, uniformly over $ h\in \mathcal{H} ,f\in \mathcal{F}$. Suppose in addition that, for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $r>0$, the localized squared-loss suprema \[ \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\ \|h-h_0\|\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[h(X)^2-h_0(X)^2]\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\ \|h-h_0\|\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{h(X_i)^2-h_0(X_i)^2\}\right| \] are measurable random variables under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. Regard the functions in $\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F$ and $\mathcal F$ as functions of $W$ through $(X,Z)$ and $Z$, respectively, and put \[ \mathcal U=\operatorname{star}(m\circ\mathcal F) \times\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F) \times\operatorname{star}(\mathcal F), \qquad \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}^2 =\mathbb E\!\left[\sum_{j=1}^3u_j(W)^2\right]. \] Define the loss with zero comparator by \[ \operatorname{clip}_B(a)=\max\{-B,\min\{a,B\}\}, \qquad \ell_B(u)=2u_1-2u_2-\operatorname{clip}_B(u_3)^2, \qquad \ell_B(0)=0. \] Suppose also that, for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $r>0$, the localized loss suprema \[ \sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\ \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(0)]\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\ \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(0)\}\right| \] are measurable random variables under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. Whenever the primal and dual roles are exchanged, suppose that these same two localized loss suprema for the exchanged problem are measurable under its fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. For iterative TRAE, suppose in addition that the following fixed-population-center squared-loss processes are measurable. For every integer $n\geqslant1$, prescribed integer $t\geqslant1$, grid point $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ for which $\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ$ is defined, radius $r>0$, and $j=1,\ldots,t$, put \[ a_{j,\lambda}(h;X) =\{h(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2 -\{h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2, \qquad d_{j,\lambda}(h;X)=\{h(X)-h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2, \] where the population iterates are defined by \eqref{eq:iter_population}. For each $v\in\{a_{j,\lambda},d_{j,\lambda}\}$, suppose that both \[ \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)v(h;X)\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i v(h;X_i)\right| \] are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, using the same function representatives as the empirical penalties. The population loss is \[ L(h) =\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb{E}\left[ 2m( W;f) -2h( X) f( Z) -f( Z)^{2}\right]. \] The TRAE estimator then minimizes the penalized empirical risk: \[ \hat{h}_{\lambda } =\arg\min_{h\in \mathcal{H}}\left\{L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]\right\}. \] → `traeEmpiricalLoss` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:32`)
- definition of the paper: We define the linear operator $ \mathcal{T} h=\Pi _{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}\mathbb{E}[ h( X) \mid Z=\cdot ]$, where $ \Pi _{\mathcal{V}}$ denotes mean-square projection onto a closed subspace $ \mathcal{V}$. → `projCondExpOp` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:117`)
- definition of the paper: where $r_0$ is the Riesz representer of the continuous linear functional $q\mapsto\mathbb E[m(W;q)]$, so that \[ \mathbb{E}[ m( W;q)] =\mathbb{E}[ r_0( Z) q( Z)] ,\quad \forall q\in \tilde{\mathcal{Q}} . \] → `momentRepresenter` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:130`)
- definition of the paper: Throughout the paper, we use $ \| \cdotp \| $ for the $ L_{2}$ norm with respect to the input distribution, i.e., \[ \| h\| :=\| h\| _{L_{2}} =\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[ h( X)^2]} ,\ \forall h\in L_{2}( X) \] and the $L_1$ norm $\|h\|_1=\mathbb E[|h(X)|]$. Write $\|h\|_{2,n}=\{\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]\}^{1/2}$ for the empirical norm. → `empNorm` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:279`)
- definition of the paper: For a function class $\mathcal F$, let $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal F)=\{\gamma f:\gamma\in[0,1],\ f\in\mathcal F\}$ and $\mathcal F-\mathcal F=\{f-f':f,f'\in\mathcal F\}$. For independent observations $X_1,\ldots,X_n$ from the input distribution, define the expected Rademacher complexity and its localized version by \begin{align*} \mathfrak R_n(\mathcal F)&=\mathbb E_{X_{1:n},\epsilon}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i f(X_i)\right|\right],\\ \mathfrak R_n(\delta;\mathcal F)&=\mathbb E_{X_{1:n},\epsilon}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal F,\,\|f\|\leqslant\delta}\left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i f(X_i)\right|\right], \end{align*} where the $\epsilon_i$ are independent Rademacher variables, independent of the observations. The localized class uses the population norm. A deterministic critical radius $\delta_{n,\mathcal F}>0$ satisfies \[ \delta_{n,\mathcal F}^2\geqslant \mathfrak R_n\bigl(\delta_{n,\mathcal F};\operatorname{star}(\mathcal F-\mathcal F)\bigr). \] For classes fitted on an independent preliminary sample, we apply this definition conditionally on that sample and use a common deterministic upper bound. → `IsCriticalRadiusBound` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:379`)
- definition of the paper: Let \[ \mathcal{H} \cdotp \mathcal{F} =\left\{(x,z)\mapsto h(x)f(z):h\in\mathcal H,f\in\mathcal F\right\} ,\qquad m\circ \mathcal{F} =\left\{w\mapsto m(w;f):f\in\mathcal F\right\} . \] → `productClass` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:351`)
- definition of the paper: Let $X$ and $Z$ be two $W$-measurable random variables, and let $ \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $ \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be closed linear subspaces of $ L_{2}( X)$ and $ L_{2}( Z)$, respectively, where $L_2(X)$ denotes square-integrable functions of $X$. Let $\mathcal{H} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $\mathcal{Q} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be subsets of these function spaces. → `IVWorld` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:22`)
- condition of the paper: Interpret the uniform almost-sure boundedness in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius} using one common null set for the whole function classes: there exists a set $N$ with $\mathbb P(W\in N)=0$ such that, for every $w\notin N$, every $h\in\mathcal H$ and every $f\in\mathcal F$, all three inequalities $|h(X(w))|\leqslant B$, $|f(Z(w))|\leqslant B$ and $|m(w;f)|\leqslant B$ hold simultaneously. The exceptional set does not depend on $h$ or $f$. Apply this convention separately to each primal or exchanged problem whenever that problem is assumed to satisfy Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}. → `TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:233`)

**Taken from the literature: bennett2023source, Lemma 9.**

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.TRAELocalizedConcentration` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:845`)

```lean
noncomputable def TRAELocalizedConcentration : Sort 0 :=
  ∀ (B : ℝ) (tildeδ : ℕ → ℝ → ℝ),
    RangeCondition V → ClosenessCondition V → MeanSquareContinuity V →
    TRAEBoundedness V B → TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness V B → TRAECriticalRadiusBound V tildeδ →
    ∃ C > 0, ∀ (δ : ℕ → ℝ) (allocation : ℕ → ℝ),
    TRAEScaleCalibration δ tildeδ allocation →
    ∀ (n : ℕ), 0 < n → allocation n ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1 →
    ∃ E : Set (Fin n → 𝒲), MeasurableSet E ∧
      1 - allocation n ≤ (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin n => V.law)).real E ∧
      ∀ x ∈ E, ∀ h ∈ V.H, ∀ f ∈ V.F,
        let u := fun w => ![V.m f w,
          primalVersion V h (V.xOf w) * dualVersion V f (V.zOf w),
          dualVersion V f (V.zOf w)]
        |(n : ℝ)⁻¹ * (∑ i, traeClippedLoss B (x i) (u (x i))) -
          (∫ w, traeClippedLoss B w (u w) ∂V.law)| ≤
          C * (δ n * (∑ i : Fin 3, inputL2Norm V.law (fun w => u w i)) + δ n ^ 2)
```

The passage of the proof:

> The localized concentration bound used in \citet[Lemma~9]{bennett2023source}
> gives
> \begin{align*}
> L( h) &=\sup_{f\in \mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[ 2( m( W;f) -f( Z) h( X)) -f^{2}( Z)\right] \\
> & =\mathbb{E}\left[ 2( m( W;f_{h}) -f_{h}( Z) h( X)) -f_{h}^{2}( Z)\right]\\
>  & \leqslant \mathbb{E}_{n}\left[ 2( m( W;f_{h}) -f_{h}( Z) h( X)) -f_{h}^{2}( Z)\right] +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2} +\delta _{n} \| f_{h} \| \right)\\
>  & \leqslant \sup_{f\in \mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[ 2( m( W;f) -f( Z) h( X)) -f^{2}( Z)\right] +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2} +\delta _{n} \| f_{h} \| \right)\\
>  & \leqslant L_{n}( h) +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2} +\delta _{n} \| f_{h} \| \right) .
> \end{align*}
> Since $\|f_h\|^2=L(h)$, Young's inequality gives
> \[
> L( h) \leqslant 2L_{n}( h) +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right) .
> \]
> Similarly, 
> \begin{align*}
> L_{n}( h)&  =\sup_{f\in \mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[ 2( m( W;f) -f( Z) h( X)) -f^{2}( Z)\right] \\
>  & \leqslant \sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\left\{\mathbb E\left[2(m(W;f)-f(Z)h(X))-f(Z)^2\right]+C\left(\delta_n^2+\delta_n\|f\|\right)\right\}\\
>  & \leqslant \sup_{f\in\widetilde{\mathcal Q}}\mathbb{E}\left[ 2( m( W;f) -f( Z) h( X)) -\frac{4}{5} f^{2}( Z)\right] +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right)\\
>  & =\frac{5}{4}\|\mathcal T(h_0-h)\|^2+O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right)\\
>  & =\frac{5}{4} L( h) +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right) .
> \end{align*}

## Lemma C.2

**Statement.** \label{lemma:strong_convexity} Under the assumptions of \cref{thm:tikhonv_rate}, on $\mathcal E_n$, the following bound holds simultaneously for all $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$: \[ L_n(\hat h_\lambda)+\frac{\lambda}{2} \|\hat h_\lambda-h_0\|^2 \leqslant C\{\delta_n^2+\|w_0\|^2\lambda^p\}, \qquad p=\min\{\beta+1,2\}. \] Moreover, for all $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$, \[ \|\hat h_\lambda-h_0\|^2 \leqslant C\left[\frac{\delta_n^2}{\lambda} +\{L_n(\hat h_\lambda)+\delta_n^2\}^{\alpha}\right], \qquad \alpha=\frac{\min\{\beta,1\}}{1+\min\{\beta,1\}}. \]

- definition of the paper: Let $X$ and $Z$ be two $W$-measurable random variables, and let $ \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $ \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be closed linear subspaces of $ L_{2}( X)$ and $ L_{2}( Z)$, respectively, where $L_2(X)$ denotes square-integrable functions of $X$. Let $\mathcal{H} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $\mathcal{Q} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be subsets of these function spaces. → `IVWorld` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:22`)
- condition of the paper: Interpret the uniform almost-sure boundedness in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius} using one common null set for the whole function classes: there exists a set $N$ with $\mathbb P(W\in N)=0$ such that, for every $w\notin N$, every $h\in\mathcal H$ and every $f\in\mathcal F$, all three inequalities $|h(X(w))|\leqslant B$, $|f(Z(w))|\leqslant B$ and $|m(w;f)|\leqslant B$ hold simultaneously. The exceptional set does not depend on $h$ or $f$. Apply this convention separately to each primal or exchanged problem whenever that problem is assumed to satisfy Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}. → `TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:233`)

**Taken from the literature: foster2023orthogonal, Lemma 14.**

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.ScalarLipschitzLocalizedConcentration` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:937`)

```lean
noncomputable def ScalarLipschitzLocalizedConcentration
    (V : IVWorld 𝒲 𝒳 𝒵) (P : Measure Ω)
    (sample : AnalysisSample V P) (split : SampleSplit) : Sort 0 :=
  IsProbabilityMeasure P →
  ∀ (B : ℝ) (tildeδ : ℕ → ℝ → ℝ),
  TRAEBoundedness V B → TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness V B → TRAECriticalRadiusBound V tildeδ →
  ∃ C > 0, ∀ (δ allocation : ℕ → ℝ),
  H0MemHypothesisClass V → TRAEScaleCalibration δ tildeδ allocation →
  ∀ n : ℕ, 0 < n → allocation n ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1 →
    ∃ E : Set Ω, MeasurableSet E ∧ 1 - allocation n ≤ P.real E ∧
      ∀ ω ∈ E, ∀ h ∈ V.H,
        |(primalEmpSq V P sample split n ω h -
            primalEmpSq V P sample split n ω (minNormSolution V)) -
          (‖h‖ ^ 2 - ‖minNormSolution V‖ ^ 2)| ≤
            C * (δ n * ‖h - minNormSolution V‖ + δ n ^ 2)
```

The passage of the proof:

> The localized squared-norm inequality
> in \citet[Lemma~14]{foster2023orthogonal} gives, uniformly over
> $h\in\mathcal H$,
> \[
> \left|\{\|h\|_{2,n}^2-\|h_0\|_{2,n}^2\}
> -\{\|h\|^2-\|h_0\|^2\}\right|
> \leqslant C\{\delta_n\|h-h_0\|+\delta_n^2\}.
> \]

- reading: The empirical loss $L_n$ is measurable, and the asserted attainment and measurable selection concern the minimum of $h\mapsto L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]$ over $h\in\mathcal H$. → `PinTRAEMinimaReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:991`)

- reading: There exists a fixed $C<\infty$, independent of $f$, $n$, and $\lambda$, such that $\mathbb E[m(W;f)^2]\leqslant C\|f\|^2$ for every $f\in\mathcal F$. → `PinMeanSquareReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:973`)
- reading: The lower bound denoted by $\Omega$ means that there exists a constant $c>0$, independent of $n$ and $\xi$, such that for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $\xi\in(0,1)$, $\tilde\delta_n(\xi)\geqslant c\sqrt{\{\log\log(en)+\log(1/\xi)\}/n}$. → `PinCriticalRadiusOmegaReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:980`)

Suppose in addition that, for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $r>0$, the localized squared-loss suprema \[ \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\ \|h-h_0\|\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[h(X)^2-h_0(X)^2]\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\ \|h-h_0\|\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{h(X_i)^2-h_0(X_i)^2\}\right| \] are measurable random variables under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. Regard the functions in $\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F$ and $\mathcal F$ as functions of $W$ through $(X,Z)$ and $Z$, respectively, and put \[ \mathcal U=\operatorname{star}(m\circ\mathcal F) \times\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F) \times\operatorname{star}(\mathcal F), \qquad \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}^2 =\mathbb E\!\left[\sum_{j=1}^3u_j(W)^2\right]. \] Define the loss with zero comparator by \[ \operatorname{clip}_B(a)=\max\{-B,\min\{a,B\}\}, \qquad \ell_B(u)=2u_1-2u_2-\operatorname{clip}_B(u_3)^2, \qquad \ell_B(0)=0. \] Suppose also that, for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $r>0$, the localized loss suprema \[ \sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\ \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(0)]\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\ \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(0)\}\right| \] are measurable random variables under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. Whenever the primal and dual roles are exchanged, suppose that these same two localized loss suprema for the exchanged problem are measurable under its fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. For iterative TRAE, suppose in addition that the following fixed-population-center squared-loss processes are measurable. For every integer $n\geqslant1$, prescribed integer $t\geqslant1$, grid point $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ for which $\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ$ is defined, radius $r>0$, and $j=1,\ldots,t$, put \[ a_{j,\lambda}(h;X) =\{h(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2 -\{h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2, \qquad d_{j,\lambda}(h;X)=\{h(X)-h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2, \] where the population iterates are defined by \eqref{eq:iter_population}. For each $v\in\{a_{j,\lambda},d_{j,\lambda}\}$, suppose that both \[ \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)v(h;X)\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i v(h;X_i)\right| \] are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, using the same function representatives as the empirical penalties. ## Lemma C.4

**Statement.** \label{lemma:trae_accepted_bounds} Suppose that the assumptions of \cref{thm:tikhonv_rate} hold. For every $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$, on $\mathcal E_n$, \begin{align*} \|\mathcal T(\hat h_\lambda-h_0)\|^2 &\leqslant C \{L_n(\hat h_\lambda)+\delta_n^2\},\\ \|\hat h_\lambda-h_0\|^2 &\leqslant C\left[V_n^{\mathrm T}(\lambda) +\{L_n(\hat h_\lambda)+\delta_n^2\}^\alpha\right], \end{align*} where the noise level and stability bound are defined in \eqref{eq:trae_common_scales} and $\alpha=\min\{\beta,1\}/\{1+\min\{\beta,1\}\}$.

Regard the functions in $\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F$ and $\mathcal F$ as functions of $W$ through $(X,Z)$ and $Z$, respectively, and put \[ \mathcal U=\operatorname{star}(m\circ\mathcal F) \times\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F) \times\operatorname{star}(\mathcal F), \qquad \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}^2 =\mathbb E\!\left[\sum_{j=1}^3u_j(W)^2\right]. \] Define the loss with zero comparator by \[ \operatorname{clip}_B(a)=\max\{-B,\min\{a,B\}\}, \qquad \ell_B(u)=2u_1-2u_2-\operatorname{clip}_B(u_3)^2, \qquad \ell_B(0)=0. \] Suppose also that, for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $r>0$, the localized loss suprema \[ \sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\ \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(0)]\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\ \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(0)\}\right| \] are measurable random variables under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. Whenever the primal and dual roles are exchanged, suppose that these same two localized loss suprema for the exchanged problem are measurable under its fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. For iterative TRAE, suppose in addition that the following fixed-population-center squared-loss processes are measurable. For every integer $n\geqslant1$, prescribed integer $t\geqslant1$, grid point $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ for which $\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ$ is defined, radius $r>0$, and $j=1,\ldots,t$, put \[ a_{j,\lambda}(h;X) =\{h(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2 -\{h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2, \qquad d_{j,\lambda}(h;X)=\{h(X)-h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2, \] where the population iterates are defined by \eqref{eq:iter_population}. For each $v\in\{a_{j,\lambda},d_{j,\lambda}\}$, suppose that both \[ \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)v(h;X)\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i v(h;X_i)\right| \] are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, using the same function representatives as the empirical penalties. - condition of the paper: We assume that the empirical loss is measurable, its minimum is attained, and the selected minimizer is measurable. → `TRAEMinimaAttained` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:735`)
- condition of the paper: In the following analyses, $\delta_n$ and $\nu_n$ denote deterministic upper bounds multiplied by fixed constants large enough to absorb the concentration and stability constants. → `CalibratedValidBounds` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:387`)
- condition of the paper: These multipliers may depend on the boundedness, density-ratio, and mean-square-continuity constants, but are independent of $n$, $\lambda$, and the unknown source parameter. → `CalibrationSourceFree` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:398`)
- condition of the paper: They are fixed before fitting the candidate estimators. → `CalibrationDeterministic` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:406`)
- definition of the paper: For a general linear moment functional $m(W;f)$, its empirical loss is \[ L_n(h) =\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[ 2m( W;f) -2h( X) f( Z) -f( Z)^{2}\right], \] where $\mathcal F\subset\tilde{\mathcal Q}$ is the test class. The population loss is \[ L(h) =\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb{E}\left[ 2m( W;f) -2h( X) f( Z) -f( Z)^{2}\right]. \] The TRAE estimator then minimizes the penalized empirical risk: \[ \hat{h}_{\lambda } =\arg\min_{h\in \mathcal{H}}\left\{L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]\right\}. \] → `traeEmpiricalLoss` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:32`)
- definition of the paper: We therefore take $D_n=L_n$ and use the common noise level and stability bound \begin{equation}\label{eq:trae_common_scales} A_n^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)=\delta_n^2, \qquad V_n^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)=\frac{\delta_n^2}{\lambda}. \end{equation} → `traeNoiseLevel` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:746`)
- definition of the paper: Fix $\lambda_0=2$ and $\rho\in[1/2,1)$, independent of $n$, and set \[ K_n=\left\lceil\frac{\log(2n^2)}{\log(1/\rho)}\right\rceil, \qquad \Lambda_n=\{\lambda_k=2\rho^k:k=0,\ldots,K_n\}. \] → `regGrid` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/Discrepancy.lean:33`)
- definition of the paper: Throughout the paper, we use $ \| \cdotp \| $ for the $ L_{2}$ norm with respect to the input distribution, i.e., \[ \| h\| :=\| h\| _{L_{2}} =\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[ h( X)^2]} ,\ \forall h\in L_{2}( X) \] and the $L_1$ norm $\|h\|_1=\mathbb E[|h(X)|]$. The same notation applies to functions of $Z$ under their input distribution. Write $\|h\|_{2,n}=\{\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]\}^{1/2}$ for the empirical norm. → `empNorm` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:279`)
- definition of the paper: Let \[ \mathcal{H} \cdotp \mathcal{F} =\left\{(x,z)\mapsto h(x)f(z):h\in\mathcal H,f\in\mathcal F\right\} ,\qquad m\circ \mathcal{F} =\left\{w\mapsto m(w;f):f\in\mathcal F\right\} . \] → `productClass` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:351`)
- definition of the paper: Let $X$ and $Z$ be two $W$-measurable random variables, and let $ \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $ \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be closed linear subspaces of $ L_{2}( X)$ and $ L_{2}( Z)$, respectively, where $L_2(X)$ denotes square-integrable functions of $X$. Let $\mathcal{H} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $\mathcal{Q} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be subsets of these function spaces. → `IVWorld` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:22`)
- condition of the paper: Interpret the uniform almost-sure boundedness in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius} using one common null set for the whole function classes: there exists a set $N$ with $\mathbb P(W\in N)=0$ such that, for every $w\notin N$, every $h\in\mathcal H$ and every $f\in\mathcal F$, all three inequalities $|h(X(w))|\leqslant B$, $|f(Z(w))|\leqslant B$ and $|m(w;f)|\leqslant B$ hold simultaneously. The exceptional set does not depend on $h$ or $f$. Apply this convention separately to each primal or exchanged problem whenever that problem is assumed to satisfy Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}. → `TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:233`)

## Lemma C.2

**Statement.** Under the assumptions of \cref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, there is a nondecreasing deterministic sequence with $b_0=0$ and $b_j\geqslant2$, $j=1,\ldots,t$, where each $b_j$ depends only on $j$ and the prescribed boundedness and mean-square-continuity constants, independently of $\beta$ and $\|w_0\|$. This sequence may be calibrated so that, on $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$, simultaneously for $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$, \begin{align} \|\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|^2 &\leqslant C\frac{b_j\delta_n^2}{\lambda^2}, \qquad j=1,\ldots,t, \label{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}\\ L(\hat h_{t,\lambda}) &\leqslant C\left[ \delta_n^2+\min\left\{\lambda, \frac{b_t\delta_n^2}{\lambda}\right\} +\|w_0\|^2\lambda^{q_t} \right]. \label{eq:iter_trae_weak_propagation} \end{align}

- definition of the paper: Fix an integer $t\geqslant1$, chosen independently of the data and the source parameter $\beta$, and initialize \[ h_{0,\lambda}^*=\hat h_{0,\lambda}=0. \] For $j=1,\ldots,t$, define the population and empirical iterates by \begin{align} h_{j,\lambda}^* &=\arg\min_{h\in\widetilde{\mathcal H}} \left\{\|\mathcal T h-r_0\|^2+\lambda\|h-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*\|^2\right\}, \label{eq:iter_population}, \\ \hat h_{j,\lambda} &=\arg\min_{h\in\mathcal H} \left\{L_n(h)+\lambda\|h-\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}\|_{2,n}^2\right\}. \label{eq:iter_rdiv_estimator} \end{align} → `iterativeRecursion` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterRDIVSetup.lean:70`)
- definition of the paper: The iterative TRAE uses the same population iterates \eqref{eq:iter_population} and initializes $\hat h_{0,\lambda}=0$. For $j=1,\ldots,t$, its empirical recursion is \[ \hat h_{j,\lambda}=\arg\min_{h\in\mathcal H}\left\{L_n(h)+\lambda\|h-\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}\|_{2,n}^2\right\}, \] where $L_n$ is the adversarial loss in \cref{sec:trae}. → `IsIterTRAEEstimator` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAECore.lean:50`)
- definition of the paper: For a general linear moment functional $m(W;f)$, its empirical loss is \[ L_n(h) =\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[ 2m( W;f) -2h( X) f( Z) -f( Z)^{2}\right], \] where $\mathcal F\subset\tilde{\mathcal Q}$ is the test class. The population loss is \[ L(h) =\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb{E}\left[ 2m( W;f) -2h( X) f( Z) -f( Z)^{2}\right]. \] The TRAE estimator then minimizes the penalized empirical risk: \[ \hat{h}_{\lambda } =\arg\min_{h\in \mathcal{H}}\left\{L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]\right\}. \] → `traeEmpiricalLoss` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:32`)
- definition of the paper: For the $t(K_n+1)$ fitted iterates, set \[ \eta_{n,t}=\frac{\xi}{t(K_n+1)} . \] → `iterGridConfidence` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/Discrepancy.lean:56`)
- definition of the paper: Let $b_t\geqslant2$ be a deterministic constant that depends on the prescribed iteration count $t$ and the fixed class bounds, independently of $\beta$ and $\|w_0\|$, and write $s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}=b_t\delta_n^2$ and $q_t=\min\{\beta+1,2t\}$. Define \begin{equation}\label{eq:iter_trae_scale} A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda) =\delta_n^2+\min\left\{\lambda,\frac{s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}}{\lambda}\right\}, \qquad V_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda) =\frac{s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}}{\lambda^2}. \end{equation} → `iterTraeNoiseLevel` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAECore.lean:95`)
- definition of the paper: For all sufficiently large $n$, let $\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ$ be the first grid point no larger than \[ r_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}} =c_{\mathrm{IT}}(s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T})^{1/(q_t+1)}, \] where $c_{\mathrm{IT}}>0$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant. → `iterTraeOraclePoint` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAECore.lean:109`)
- definition of the paper: Throughout the paper, we use $ \| \cdotp \| $ for the $ L_{2}$ norm with respect to the input distribution, i.e., \[ \| h\| :=\| h\| _{L_{2}} =\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[ h( X)^2]} ,\ \forall h\in L_{2}( X) \] and the $L_1$ norm $\|h\|_1=\mathbb E[|h(X)|]$. The same notation applies to functions of $Z$ under their input distribution. Write $\|h\|_{2,n}=\{\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]\}^{1/2}$ for the empirical norm. → `empNorm` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:279`)
- definition of the paper: For a function class $\mathcal F$, let $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal F)=\{\gamma f:\gamma\in[0,1],\ f\in\mathcal F\}$ and $\mathcal F-\mathcal F=\{f-f':f,f'\in\mathcal F\}$. For independent observations $X_1,\ldots,X_n$ from the input distribution, define the expected Rademacher complexity and its localized version by \begin{align*} \mathfrak R_n(\mathcal F)&=\mathbb E_{X_{1:n},\epsilon}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i f(X_i)\right|\right],\\ \mathfrak R_n(\delta;\mathcal F)&=\mathbb E_{X_{1:n},\epsilon}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal F,\,\|f\|\leqslant\delta}\left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i f(X_i)\right|\right], \end{align*} where the $\epsilon_i$ are independent Rademacher variables, independent of the observations. The localized class uses the population norm. A deterministic critical radius $\delta_{n,\mathcal F}>0$ satisfies \[ \delta_{n,\mathcal F}^2\geqslant \mathfrak R_n\bigl(\delta_{n,\mathcal F};\operatorname{star}(\mathcal F-\mathcal F)\bigr). \] For classes fitted on an independent preliminary sample, we apply this definition conditionally on that sample and use a common deterministic upper bound. → `IsCriticalRadiusBound` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:379`)
- definition of the paper: Let \[ \mathcal{H} \cdotp \mathcal{F} =\left\{(x,z)\mapsto h(x)f(z):h\in\mathcal H,f\in\mathcal F\right\} ,\qquad m\circ \mathcal{F} =\left\{w\mapsto m(w;f):f\in\mathcal F\right\} . \] → `productClass` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:351`)
- definition of the paper: Fix $\lambda_0=2$ and $\rho\in[1/2,1)$, independent of $n$, and set \[ K_n=\left\lceil\frac{\log(2n^2)}{\log(1/\rho)}\right\rceil, \qquad \Lambda_n=\{\lambda_k=2\rho^k:k=0,\ldots,K_n\}. \] → `regGrid` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/Discrepancy.lean:33`)
- definition of the paper: Let $X$ and $Z$ be two $W$-measurable random variables, and let $ \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $ \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be closed linear subspaces of $ L_{2}( X)$ and $ L_{2}( Z)$, respectively, where $L_2(X)$ denotes square-integrable functions of $X$. Let $\mathcal{H} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $\mathcal{Q} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be subsets of these function spaces. → `IVWorld` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:22`)
- condition of the paper: Interpret the uniform almost-sure boundedness in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius} using one common null set for the whole function classes: there exists a set $N$ with $\mathbb P(W\in N)=0$ such that, for every $w\notin N$, every $h\in\mathcal H$ and every $f\in\mathcal F$, all three inequalities $|h(X(w))|\leqslant B$, $|f(Z(w))|\leqslant B$ and $|m(w;f)|\leqslant B$ hold simultaneously. The exceptional set does not depend on $h$ or $f$. Apply this convention separately to each primal or exchanged problem whenever that problem is assumed to satisfy Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}. → `TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:233`)

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.IterTRAEAnchoredLocalizedConcentration` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAEConcentration.lean:92`)

```lean
noncomputable def IterTRAEAnchoredLocalizedConcentration : Sort 0 :=
  ∀ B cfloor : ℝ, TRAEBoundedness V B → TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness V B → 0 < cfloor →
  ∃ C > 0, ∀ n : ℕ, 3 ≤ n → ∀ ζ ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1,
  ∀ r : ℝ, cfloor * Real.sqrt
    ((3 * Real.log (Real.log n) + Real.log (1 / ζ)) / n) ≤ r →
  0 < r →
  (∀ F ∈ ({starClass (momentImageClass V),
      starClass (productObservationClass V), starClass (criticObservationClass V)} :
        Set (Set (𝒲 → ℝ))),
    IsCriticalRadiusBound V.law F n r) →
  ∀ (ρ c b β : ℝ) (δ : ℕ → ℝ) (t : ℕ),
  GridDomain ρ → 0 < c → 2 ≤ b → 0 < β → 1 ≤ t →
  let radius := c * (b * δ n ^ 2) ^ (1 / (qualificationExponent β t + 1))
  ∀ anchor : 𝒲 → Fin 3 → ℝ,
  (anchor = 0 ∨ ∃ j : ℕ, 1 ≤ j ∧ j ≤ t ∧
    ∃ lam ∈ regGrid ρ n,
      (∃ k ≤ terminalIndex ρ n, gridValue ρ k ≤ radius) ∧
      oraclePoint ρ n radius ≤ lam ∧ lam ≤ 2 ∧ 0 < lam ∧
      populationIterate V lam j ∈ V.H ∧
      projCondExpOp V (minNormSolution V - populationIterate V lam j) ∈ V.F ∧
      anchor = iterTraePopulationAnchor V j lam) →
  anchor ∈ traeLossClass V →
  AELocalizedSupremaMeasurableAt V.law (traeLossClass V) anchor (traeClippedLoss B) n →
  ∃ E : Set (Fin n → 𝒲), MeasurableSet E ∧
    1 - ζ ≤ (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin n => V.law)).real E ∧
    ∀ x ∈ E, ∀ u ∈ traeLossClass V,
      |(n : ℝ)⁻¹ * (∑ i, (traeClippedLoss B (x i) (u (x i)) -
          traeClippedLoss B (x i) (anchor (x i)))) -
        ∫ w, (traeClippedLoss B w (u w) - traeClippedLoss B w (anchor w)) ∂V.law| ≤
      C * (r * (∑ i : Fin 3, inputL2Norm V.law (fun w => u w i - anchor w i)) + r ^ 2)
```

The passage of the proof:

> Apply the localized concentration inequality of \citet[Lemma~9]{bennett2023source} to the product and moment-image classes in \cref{asp:critical_radius}, and apply its bounded Lipschitz version to the squared test functions.

```lean
noncomputable def IterTRAEFixedCenterSquaredLossConcentration : Sort 0 :=
  ∀ B cfloor : ℝ, TRAEBoundedness V B → TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness V B → 0 < cfloor →
  ∃ C > 0, ∀ n : ℕ, 3 ≤ n → ∀ ζ ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1,
  ∀ r : ℝ, cfloor * Real.sqrt
    ((Real.log (Real.log n) + Real.log (1 / ζ)) / n) ≤ r → 0 < r →
  ∀ (F : Set (𝒲 → ℝ)) (anchor center : 𝒲 → ℝ),
  IterTRAEScalarSourceApplication V n F anchor center →
  anchor ∈ F →
  (∀ᵐ w ∂V.law, ∀ f ∈ F, |f w| ≤ 2 * max 1 B) →
  (∀ w (u v : Fin 1 → ℝ),
    |iterTraeSourceSquare B center w u - iterTraeSourceSquare B center w v| ≤
      |u 0 - v 0|) →
  expectedRademacher V.law {f ∈ starClass F | eLpNorm f 2 V.law ≤ ENNReal.ofReal r} n ≤
    ENNReal.ofReal (r ^ 2) →
  AELocalizedSupremaMeasurableAt V.law (iterTraeScalarClass F)
    (fun w => fun _ : Fin 1 => anchor w) (iterTraeSourceSquare B center) n →
  ∃ E : Set (Fin n → 𝒲), MeasurableSet E ∧
    1 - ζ ≤ (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin n => V.law)).real E ∧
    ∀ x ∈ E, ∀ f ∈ F,
      |(n : ℝ)⁻¹ * (∑ i,
          (iterTraeSourceSquare B center (x i) (fun _ => f (x i)) -
           iterTraeSourceSquare B center (x i) (fun _ => anchor (x i)))) -
        ∫ w, (iterTraeSourceSquare B center w (fun _ => f w) -
          iterTraeSourceSquare B center w (fun _ => anchor w)) ∂V.law| ≤
      C * (r * inputL2Norm V.law (fun w => f w - anchor w) + r ^ 2)
```

The passage of the proof:

> For the fixed-center squared-loss comparisons, apply the same localized concentration inequality of \citet[Lemma~9]{bennett2023source} to the bounded clipped losses corresponding to $(h-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*)^2$ at comparator $h_{j,\lambda}^*$ and $(h-h_{k,\lambda}^*)^2$ at comparator $h_{k,\lambda}^*$, for the fixed iteration--grid pairs under consideration.

## Corollary C.6

**Statement.** \label{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound} Under the assumptions of \cref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, the calibrated iterative TRAE scales satisfy \eqref{eq:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound} simultaneously for all $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ on $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$.

- side condition: With $\mathcal U$ and $\ell_B$ as in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}, for every fixed iteration--grid pair $(j,\lambda)$ under consideration and every $r>0$, both $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)\{\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W))\}|$ and $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W_i))\}|$ are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, where $f_j^*=\mathcal T(h_0-h_{j,\lambda}^*)$ and $u_{j,\lambda}^*(W)=(m(W;f_j^*),h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)f_j^*(Z),f_j^*(Z))$. → `IterTRAEAnchoredLossProcessesMeasurable` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAECore.lean:27`)
- reading: The empirical loss $L_n$ is measurable, and the asserted attainment and measurable selection concern the minimum of $h\mapsto L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]$ over $h\in\mathcal H$. → `PinTRAEMinimaReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:991`)
- reading: Under the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, the displayed inequality holds simultaneously for all $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ on $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$, where $\mathbb P(\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}})\geqslant1-\xi$. → `PinIterTRAEEnvelopeReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAEPropagation.lean:377`)
- reading: There exists a fixed $C<\infty$, independent of $f$, $n$, and $\lambda$, such that $\mathbb E[m(W;f)^2]\leqslant C\|f\|^2$ for every $f\in\mathcal F$. → `PinMeanSquareReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:973`)
- reading: The lower bound denoted by $\Omega$ means that there exists a constant $c>0$, independent of $n$ and $\xi$, such that for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $\xi\in(0,1)$, $\tilde\delta_n(\xi)\geqslant c\sqrt{\{\log\log(en)+\log(1/\xi)\}/n}$. → `PinCriticalRadiusOmegaReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:980`)
- condition of the paper: \label{asp:range} We have $ r_{0} \in \mathcal{R}(\mathcal{T}) :=\{\mathcal{T} h:h\in \tilde{\mathcal{H}}\}$. → `RangeCondition` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:136`)
- condition of the paper: \label{asp:source_condition} Problem (\ref{eq:lin_inv_prob}) satisfies the $ \beta $-source condition for some $ \beta >0$, i.e., there exists $ w_{0} \in \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ such that the minimum-norm solution of (\ref{eq:lin_inv_prob}) satisfies $ h_{0} =\left(\mathcal{T}^{*}\mathcal{T}\right)^{\beta /2} w_{0}$. → `BetaSourceCondition` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:203`)

- condition of the paper: For a fixed $B<\infty$, suppose that $|m(W;f)|$, $|h(X)|$, and $|f(Z)|$ are a.s. bounded by $B$, uniformly over $ h\in \mathcal{H} ,f\in \mathcal{F}$. Suppose in addition that, for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $r>0$, the localized squared-loss suprema \[ \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\ \|h-h_0\|\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[h(X)^2-h_0(X)^2]\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\ \|h-h_0\|\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{h(X_i)^2-h_0(X_i)^2\}\right| \] are measurable random variables under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. Regard the functions in $\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F$ and $\mathcal F$ as functions of $W$ through $(X,Z)$ and $Z$, respectively, and put \[ \mathcal U=\operatorname{star}(m\circ\mathcal F) \times\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F) \times\operatorname{star}(\mathcal F), \qquad \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}^2 =\mathbb E\!\left[\sum_{j=1}^3u_j(W)^2\right]. \] Define the loss with zero comparator by \[ \operatorname{clip}_B(a)=\max\{-B,\min\{a,B\}\}, \qquad \ell_B(u)=2u_1-2u_2-\operatorname{clip}_B(u_3)^2, \qquad \ell_B(0)=0. \] Suppose also that, for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $r>0$, the localized loss suprema \[ \sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\ \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(0)]\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\ \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(0)\}\right| \] are measurable random variables under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. Whenever the primal and dual roles are exchanged, suppose that these same two localized loss suprema for the exchanged problem are measurable under its fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. For iterative TRAE, suppose in addition that the following fixed-population-center squared-loss processes are measurable. For every integer $n\geqslant1$, prescribed integer $t\geqslant1$, grid point $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ for which $\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ$ is defined, radius $r>0$, and $j=1,\ldots,t$, put \[ a_{j,\lambda}(h;X) =\{h(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2 -\{h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2, \qquad d_{j,\lambda}(h;X)=\{h(X)-h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2, \] where the population iterates are defined by \eqref{eq:iter_population}. For each $v\in\{a_{j,\lambda},d_{j,\lambda}\}$, suppose that both \[ \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)v(h;X)\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i v(h;X_i)\right| \] are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, using the same function representatives as the empirical penalties. The zero-iterate comparison uses $\hat h_{0,\lambda}=h_{0,\lambda}^*=0$. → `TRAEBoundedness` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:221`)
- condition of the paper: $ \tilde{\delta}_{n}(\xi) = \Omega \left(\sqrt{(\log\log(en) +\log( 1/\xi )) /n}\right)$ is a common upper bound on the critical radii of $ \operatorname{star}(\mathcal{H} \cdotp \mathcal{F})$, $ \operatorname{star}( m\circ \mathcal{F})$, $ \operatorname{star}(\mathcal{F})$, $ \operatorname{star}(\mathcal{H})$, and $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H-\mathcal H)$. → `TRAECriticalRadiusBound` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:706`)
- condition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:delta_trae} \delta_n \geqslant \max\{\tilde{\delta}_n(\eta_n),n^{-1/2}\} = \Omega \left(\sqrt{\frac{\log\log(en)+\log((K_n+1)/\xi)}{n}}\right), \qquad \delta_n=o(1). \end{equation} → `TRAEScaleCalibration` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:722`)
- condition of the paper: We assume that the empirical loss is measurable, its minimum is attained, and the selected minimizer is measurable. → `TRAEMinimaAttained` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:735`)
- condition of the paper: In the following analyses, $\delta_n$ and $\nu_n$ denote deterministic upper bounds multiplied by fixed constants large enough to absorb the concentration and stability constants. → `CalibratedValidBounds` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:387`)
- condition of the paper: These multipliers may depend on the boundedness, density-ratio, and mean-square-continuity constants, but are independent of $n$, $\lambda$, and the unknown source parameter. → `CalibrationSourceFree` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:398`)
- condition of the paper: They are fixed before fitting the candidate estimators. → `CalibrationDeterministic` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:406`)
- condition of the paper: \label{asp:iter_realizability_trae} For every $n$ for which $\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ$ is defined, $h_{j,\lambda}^*\in\mathcal H$ for $j=1,\ldots,t$ and $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$. → `IterRealizableTRAE` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAECore.lean:185`)
- condition of the paper: \label{asp:iter_mean_sq_continuity} For all $f,f'\in\mathcal F$, \[ \mathbb{E}\left[m(W;f-f')^{2}\right]\leqslant C\|f-f'\|^{2} \] for a fixed constant $C<\infty$. → `IterMeanSquareContinuity` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAECore.lean:74`)
- condition of the paper: As for iterative RDIV, each empirical minimum is attained and the selected minimizers are measurable. → `IterTRAEMinimaAttained` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAECore.lean:62`)
- definition of the paper: The iterative TRAE uses the same population iterates \eqref{eq:iter_population} and initializes $\hat h_{0,\lambda}=0$. For $j=1,\ldots,t$, its empirical recursion is \[ \hat h_{j,\lambda}=\arg\min_{h\in\mathcal H}\left\{L_n(h)+\lambda\|h-\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}\|_{2,n}^2\right\}, \] where $L_n$ is the adversarial loss in \cref{sec:trae}. → `IsIterTRAEEstimator` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAECore.lean:50`)
- definition of the paper: For a general linear moment functional $m(W;f)$, its empirical loss is \[ L_n(h) =\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[ 2m( W;f) -2h( X) f( Z) -f( Z)^{2}\right], \] where $\mathcal F\subset\tilde{\mathcal Q}$ is the test class. The population loss is \[ L(h) =\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb{E}\left[ 2m( W;f) -2h( X) f( Z) -f( Z)^{2}\right]. \] The TRAE estimator then minimizes the penalized empirical risk: \[ \hat{h}_{\lambda } =\arg\min_{h\in \mathcal{H}}\left\{L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]\right\}. \] → `traeEmpiricalLoss` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:32`)
- definition of the paper: We therefore take $D_n=L_n$ and use the common noise level and stability bound \begin{equation}\label{eq:trae_common_scales} A_n^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)=\delta_n^2, \qquad V_n^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)=\frac{\delta_n^2}{\lambda}. \end{equation} → `traeNoiseLevel` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:746`)
- definition of the paper: Let $b_t\geqslant2$ be a deterministic constant that depends on the prescribed iteration count $t$ and the fixed class bounds, independently of $\beta$ and $\|w_0\|$, and write $s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}=b_t\delta_n^2$ and $q_t=\min\{\beta+1,2t\}$. Define \begin{equation}\label{eq:iter_trae_scale} A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda) =\delta_n^2+\min\left\{\lambda,\frac{s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}}{\lambda}\right\}, \qquad V_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda) =\frac{s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}}{\lambda^2}. \end{equation} → `iterTraeNoiseLevel` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAECore.lean:95`)
- definition of the paper: \begin{equation}\label{eq:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound} L_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda}) \leqslant A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda) +C_{\mathrm{IT}}\|w_0\|^2\lambda^{q_t}, \qquad \lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]. \end{equation} → `IterTRAEDiscrepancyEnvelope` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAECore.lean:232`)
- definition of the paper: For all sufficiently large $n$, let $\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ$ be the first grid point no larger than \[ r_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}} =c_{\mathrm{IT}}(s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T})^{1/(q_t+1)}, \] where $c_{\mathrm{IT}}>0$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant. → `iterTraeOraclePoint` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAECore.lean:109`)
- definition of the paper: Fix $\lambda_0=2$ and $\rho\in[1/2,1)$, independent of $n$, and set \[ K_n=\left\lceil\frac{\log(2n^2)}{\log(1/\rho)}\right\rceil, \qquad \Lambda_n=\{\lambda_k=2\rho^k:k=0,\ldots,K_n\}. \] → `regGrid` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/Discrepancy.lean:33`)
- definition of the paper: Let \[ \mathcal{H} \cdotp \mathcal{F} =\left\{(x,z)\mapsto h(x)f(z):h\in\mathcal H,f\in\mathcal F\right\} ,\qquad m\circ \mathcal{F} =\left\{w\mapsto m(w;f):f\in\mathcal F\right\} . \] → `productClass` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:351`)
- definition of the paper: Let $X$ and $Z$ be two $W$-measurable random variables, and let $ \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $ \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be closed linear subspaces of $ L_{2}( X)$ and $ L_{2}( Z)$, respectively, where $L_2(X)$ denotes square-integrable functions of $X$. Let $\mathcal{H} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{H}}$ and $\mathcal{Q} \subset \tilde{\mathcal{Q}}$ be subsets of these function spaces. → `IVWorld` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:22`)
- condition of the paper: Interpret the uniform almost-sure boundedness in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius} using one common null set for the whole function classes: there exists a set $N$ with $\mathbb P(W\in N)=0$ such that, for every $w\notin N$, every $h\in\mathcal H$ and every $f\in\mathcal F$, all three inequalities $|h(X(w))|\leqslant B$, $|f(Z(w))|\leqslant B$ and $|m(w;f)|\leqslant B$ hold simultaneously. The exceptional set does not depend on $h$ or $f$. Apply this convention separately to each primal or exchanged problem whenever that problem is assumed to satisfy Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}. → `TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:233`)

## Lemma C.7

**Statement.** \label{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability} Let $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ satisfy $\lambda\geqslant\sqrt{s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}}$. On $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$, \begin{align} &\|\mathcal T(\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_0)\|^2 \leqslant C_t \{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}, \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_total}\\ &\|\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_0\|^2 \leqslant C_t\left[ \frac{1}{\lambda}\{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\} +\{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}^{\beta/(\beta+1)} \right], \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_total_strong}\\ &\lambda\|\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_{t,\lambda}^*\|^2 +\|\mathcal T(\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_{t,\lambda}^*)\|^2 \leqslant C_t \{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}, \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_variance}\\ &\|\mathcal T(h_{t,\lambda}^*-h_0)\|^2 \leqslant C_t \{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}, \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_bias}\\ &\|\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_{t,\lambda}^*\|^2 \leqslant \frac{C_t}{\lambda} \{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}. \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_strong} \end{align}

**Conditions and readings, and the Lean definitions that carry them.**

- side condition: With $\mathcal U$ and $\ell_B$ as in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}, for every fixed iteration--grid pair $(j,\lambda)$ under consideration and every $r>0$, both $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)\{\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W))\}|$ and $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W_i))\}|$ are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, where $f_j^*=\mathcal T(h_0-h_{j,\lambda}^*)$ and $u_{j,\lambda}^*(W)=(m(W;f_j^*),h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)f_j^*(Z),f_j^*(Z))$. → `IterTRAEAnchoredLossProcessesMeasurable` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAECore.lean:27`)
- reading: The empirical loss $L_n$ is measurable, and the asserted attainment and measurable selection concern the minimum of $h\mapsto L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]$ over $h\in\mathcal H$. → `PinTRAEMinimaReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:991`)
- reading: The constant $C_t$ may depend on the prescribed $t$ and fixed population quantities, including $\beta$ and $\|w_0\|$, but is independent of $n$, $\lambda$, and the realized fitting observations. → `PinIterTRAEAcceptedConstant` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/IterTRAEStability.lean:225`)
- reading: There exists a fixed $C<\infty$, independent of $f$, $n$, and $\lambda$, such that $\mathbb E[m(W;f)^2]\leqslant C\|f\|^2$ for every $f\in\mathcal F$. → `PinMeanSquareReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:973`)
- reading: The lower bound denoted by $\Omega$ means that there exists a constant $c>0$, independent of $n$ and $\xi$, such that for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $\xi\in(0,1)$, $\tilde\delta_n(\xi)\geqslant c\sqrt{\{\log\log(en)+\log(1/\xi)\}/n}$. → `PinCriticalRadiusOmegaReading` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:980`)
- condition of the paper: \label{asp:range} We have $ r_{0} \in \mathcal{R}(\mathcal{T}) :=\{\mathcal{T} h:h\in \tilde{\mathcal{H}}\}$. → `RangeCondition` (`lean/Papers/TsAdaptiveEstimationInference/Basic.lean:136`)
